```
Use the Magic Headline Formula to write 10 product name options for [PRODUCT]. Each name should state a specific result and a defined timeframe in which the customer gets it. Then write a product description for the best option in the same formula, aimed at [TARGET CUSTOMER] who has [PROBLEM]. Keep the language plain and concrete.
```

[L0683.020]

- A name can carry the whole concept when it describes the buyer's situation or payoff in a few words. A name that expresses a use or a feeling does part of the selling before the copy starts. [L1366.030, L1366.106]
- Name variants with a story or setting rather than plain descriptors, so the product sells a feeling and a picture of how the buyer will feel. A plain scent name becomes a named outdoor or evocative variant with a short descriptive label. [L0524.010]
- Use plain, descriptive words that state what the item actually is, instead of clever brand names, for products and funnel stages. Buyers scan for their exact problem, so a precise name often converts better than a creative one. [L1016.073]
- Where the two naming approaches differ: concept or story names fit products sold on a feeling or payoff [L1366.030, L0524.010], while plain names fit products and funnel stages where buyers scan for the exact problem they have [L1016.073].
- A unique product name is less competitive in Google search than a generic product name, so buyers can find the product again by searching for it. Use a distinctive name even when the market uses generic terms. Custom wording can also be less competitive than generic supplier product titles. [L1209.001, L1210.023]

### Product descriptions

- Short, simple and attractive descriptions often convert better than long technical ones because they intrigue the buyer more. [L0513.006]
- Feature-only copy can work in a crowded market when the feature proves superior quality or performance against competitors. The copy has to show why the product is better than alternatives, not just list specifications. [L0919.012]
- Emphasise, in order of clarity: the features, the benefits for the customer, and the technology or method behind the product. It works because customers buy outcomes, and the technology explains why the product delivers them. [L1071.017]
  - Example: a water bottle description names its insulation feature, what it means for daily use, and how it keeps drinks cold for hours. [L1071.017]
  - Example: a kitchen knife description names its steel, the cutting benefit, and the sharpening method. [L1071.017]
- Turn a feature into a benefit by naming the good quality and the drawback it avoids, in plain words rather than technical terms. Examples: *Easy movement without the drag* and *Keep-you-moving support, nothing stiff about it*. [L0884.019]
- Explain the materials and why they cause comfort or recovery, so the buyer understands a higher price. Buyers pay more when they can see the reason for the value. [L1194.045]
- Every product is two products: the physical product (materials and construction) and the functional product (what it does for the buyer). Buyers pay for the functional product; the physical product matters because it makes the functional product possible. [L0330.010]
- Use physical features in copy in four ways:
  1. To justify the price.
  2. To document the quality of the performance.
  3. To assure the performance will last over years.
  4. To sharpen the buyer's mental picture of the performance.

  The most important use is to give a new claim fresh believability, by introducing a new mechanism, quality or freedom from an old limitation that others lack. [L0330.011]
- For high page-count books, state the page count in the short product description next to the bundle name and price. The page count justifies a higher price to the buyer. [L0354.024]
- Choosing the description length:

  | Situation | Approach | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | General case | Short, simple, attractive; often converts better | [L0513.006] |
  | Crowded market, feature is measurably superior | Prove the feature against alternatives | [L0919.012] |
  | Technology or method explains the result | Features, then benefits, then the method | [L1071.017] |
  | Product needs education | Up to three or four lines of copy | [L1345.045] |

### Headlines, subheadlines and taglines

- Two-part tagline: an action followed by a reassuring outcome, kept to four words or fewer. The contrast gives the reader a feeling and a reason in one line, which makes it memorable and easy to repeat. Examples: *Train hard, recover faster.* and *Dig deep, sleep well.* [L0524.077]
- Product page headline pattern: name a hidden root cause, promise one simple daily action that addresses it, then list three to four outcomes the buyer feels. It gives the visitor a reason the problem persists and a single easy step to fix it. [L1164.058]
  - Example: *Cuts the hidden late-day slump that drains your focus, so afternoons feel sharp with one stick pack a day.* [L1164.058]
  - Example: *Eases the hidden tension that builds up at a desk, so you can work comfortably with one daily routine.* [L1164.058]
- Subheadline formula: product name, then a one-line subheadline that names the exact problem the buyer has and the result the product delivers. Mobile visitors decide within seconds whether a page is meant for them, and a specific pain point signals that it is. [L1260.063]
  - Example: *Sore feet after a 10-hour shift? Cushioned insoles built for all-day standing.* [L1260.063]
  - Example: *Winter-dry skin that cracks by Tuesday? A rich hand cream for daily use.* [L1260.063]
- Semi-headline pattern: a one-line, benefit-driven line placed directly under the product name, naming the exact pain point of the target customer and what the product does about it. It tells a visitor who has just landed what the product is. [L1345.044]
  - Example: *Tired of cold feet on winter mornings? Heated insoles that warm up in 30 seconds.* [L1345.044]
  - Example: *Losing sleep to snoring? A chin strap that keeps your airway open all night.* [L1345.044]
- Copy structure: one benefit-driven headline line, then a paragraph of no more than two lines. Long paragraphs make visitors leave, so only complicated products that need education can use three or four lines. [L1345.045]
- Promise line under the product name, with the result, the time it takes and the guarantee in one short line. It states the value, sets the buyer's expectation and removes risk. Use this structure for any problem-solving product. [L0871.060]
  - Example: *Relieve back tension in 10 minutes a day or your money back.* [L0871.060]
  - Example: *Sleep through the night with less neck pain in 7 days, or get a full refund.* [L0871.060]
- Opening line formula for a subject line or first line: **[Activity or task] has never been this easy** (or *has never been easier*). It makes the reader curious about how much effort is saved and speaks to the use case rather than pushing a sale. [L0605.045]
  - Example for a fitness product: *Your evening workout has never been this easy.* [L0605.045]
  - Example for a kitchen product: *Breakfast prep has never been this quick.* [L0605.045]
- Welcome email subject lines should combine a benefit of joining with an offer, and use curiosity-driven lines that speak to the use case. [L0605.048]

### Problem, agitate, solution and differentiation

- Problem-agitate-solution pattern:
  1. State the customer's problem.
  2. Make the pain of that problem vivid.
  3. Present the product as the solution.

  [L0552.029]
  - Example: *Tired of cold coffee by the time you reach your desk? Our insulated mug keeps drinks hot for hours, so every sip is still warm.* [L0552.029]
  - Example: *Sick of tangled cables on your desk? The velcro cable organiser keeps every charger in place, so your workspace looks clean in seconds.* [L0552.029]
- Escalate everyday pain with concrete, sensory scenes, such as hair found in breakfast, in coffee or in the mouth. Specific, vivid moments make the problem feel personal and push the customer toward a solution. [L0450.035]
- The satisfying feel of the action, which buyers mention in reviews, is an emotional benefit. Use it in copy alongside the functional benefit. Describing how the product feels to use can differentiate it from competitors who only list functions. [L0450.036]
- For a contractor or DIY audience, name the pain points directly: planning takes too long, generic guides skip real steps, and sourcing materials decides success. Then promise verified steps, specific sourcing and a faster start. [L0794.026]
- Before writing direct-response landing page copy, list every problem the customer goes through, including the tension and the emotional motivation behind each one. For example, a dirty house matters because it feels unhygienic and embarrassing. [L0917.004]
- Differentiation pattern: when a product competes with similar tools, lead with the unique feature and the measurable benefit, not the generic job the product does.
  - Example: *Our kitchen knife stays sharp for 3 times longer than standard steel blades, so you cut less and spend less time sharpening.* [L0552.030]
  - Example: *Our running shoe weighs 180 grams, half the weight of typical trainers, so every stride feels lighter.* [L0552.030]

### Concept-led selling and emotional journeys

- Build every sales letter around one core belief with three parts: a new opportunity, the reader's desire it unlocks, and a new mechanism that only you can provide. Write the belief down before drafting, because it becomes the mission and common thread that keeps all copy focused. [L1365.001]

  | Part | Role in the belief |
  |---|---|
  | New opportunity | The thing that is new for the reader |
  | Desire | What the reader wants it to unlock |
  | New mechanism | The way only you can deliver it |

  [L1365.001]

- Belief formula:

  ```
  One belief = [New opportunity] is the key to [prospect's desire] and it's attainable only through [new mechanism].
  ```

  Example: *Fixing evening light exposure is the key to falling asleep faster and waking rested, and it's attainable only through our timed light-and-temperature routine.* [L1365.002]

- The desire must be tied to a benefit that lets the prospect feel more significant, respected or valued, improves their mental, physical, social, emotional, sexual or financial well-being, or reduces the risk of events that would harm these areas. A new opportunity only works as the key to a desire when it delivers one of these benefits. [L1365.004]
- Typical desires by niche:
  - Health: losing weight, lowering blood pressure, building muscle.
  - Business opportunity: working from home, quitting a job.
  - Financial: a secure retirement, taking care of family. [L1365.005]
- Emotional journey positioning, in six steps:
  1. State what the product is not, to challenge market assumptions.
  2. State what it is, as an emotional metaphor or transformation.
  3. Describe the journey it enables between emotional states.
  4. Name it after the emotional state it creates, not its function.
  5. Connect that journey to how the user aspires to move through their own life.
  6. End with the emotional destination the buyer will reach.

  It works because it bypasses rational objection and engages the emotional core of all purchase decisions. [L0196.066]
  - Example for an email management tool: *This isn't another inbox. It's your return to focus. It carries you from buried to sovereign. We call it Clarity. It moves the way leaders move, with intention. A place where your attention belongs to you again.* [L0196.066]
  - Example for a sleep tracking app: *This isn't just data. It's your body talking back. It journeys with you from exhausted to restored. We call it Rhythm. It guides the way wellness guides, with rhythm. A place where you remember what rest feels like.* [L0196.066]
- Customers buy the outcome they want, and the product is only the vehicle. Ask buyers what result they want, such as less stress, better behaviour, more confidence or better communication, instead of asking only what they think of the product. This outcome language is what sells. [L0906.003]
- People buy emotions, identity and vibes, not products. The sales message should make the buyer feel comfort, coziness or belonging. A product that fits a mood sells faster than one described by features. [L0347.027]
- Copywriting should communicate how the product improves the customer's life and trigger emotion, not only list features such as quality or shipping time. Emotional, benefit-driven language helps customers connect with the product and lifts add-to-cart and checkout rates. [L0773.019]
- A useful copywriting framework from *100 Million Offers* by Alex Hormozi: show the customer's problem, the perceived likelihood of success, and the value delivered, including how quickly and with how little effort the customer reaches the goal. [L0773.021]
- Products sell more easily when they create an emotional connection or trigger a reaction in the buyer. A random product with no story or meaning is harder to sell. [L1219.023]
- Not every product needs an emotional angle. If the product has no emotional meaning, put the emphasis on the specific problem it solves and how it solves it. [L1219.026]
- Feature-led copy and emotional copy meet in one rule: the product is only worth the emotion if the copy proves the result. [L0906.003, L1219.026]
- Give a gift angle, such as a perfect holiday gift, to give buyers a second reason to purchase. It works best with products that suit the holiday season. [L1194.046]

### Storytelling and credibility

- Credibility comes from three storylines:
  1. *I've been in your shoes*: the strongest, where the author faced the same struggle and found the mechanism.
  2. The Robin Hood storyline: the author reveals a secret usually kept from ordinary people.
  3. The expert storyline: credentials such as books, TV appearances, years of experience and accomplishments.

  Use the first option if possible, fall back to the others when it is not, and mix them when that makes the story stronger. [L1365.027]
- Story formula: describe a struggle that mirrors the reader's current pain, then the moment of discovering your mechanism, then the result the reader wants, achieved. Include relatable background details such as a middle-class upbringing or limited starting money. The reader sees their own life in the author and imagines the same outcome without the seller saying it directly. [L1365.028]
  - Example: *I was paying for three ad tools and still seeing no sales, until I simplified my funnel to one page; within a quarter the store was profitable on half the budget.* [L1365.028]
  - Example: *I worked two jobs and still had no savings, until one change to my weekly budget routine let me pay off my card in eight months.* [L1365.028]
- Insider-secret formula: describe an insider secret that the powerful use, state that you are revealing it, and promise the reader the same edge. It works best when the secret is something they do not want the reader to know. It positions the seller as an ally who levels the playing field against insiders. [L1365.029]
  - Example: *I spent years on the buying side of this industry and watched how big suppliers quietly steer everyone toward the most expensive tier; here is the pricing trick I kept to myself.* [L1365.029]
  - Example: *Before I left the agency world, I saw which accounts got the cheapest clicks; the method they never taught clients is what I'm sharing here.* [L1365.029]
- For a product that needs education, lead with the problem and story rather than the product box. Well-known brands can lead with the product because the audience already knows them. [L1225.043]
- Believability matters more than credibility. Facts such as years in business or number of clients build trust, but customer stories, benefits achieved and doubts resolved persuade more strongly. Facts only support persuasion and do not replace it. [L1370.107]

### Email and community messaging

- Choose the community word that fits the niche: *community* or *family* for jewellery, *club* for passionate niches such as fitness, and *family* for pet niches. Use it in the welcome email so new subscribers feel they are joining a group. [L0605.046]
- Add a short brand story to the footer of welcome emails, such as an owner story or any story about the store, if you do not want to share a personal name. Customers relate to it and trust the store more. [L0605.047]
- Welcome email subject lines should combine a benefit of joining with an offer, and use curiosity-driven lines that speak to the use case. [L0605.048]

### Restraint and tone limits

- Keep to the niche's tone rules: emojis only for playful brands, no slang in site copy, no dropshipping in consumer copy, and a quiet premium tone instead of the word luxury. [L0853.043, L1467.004, L0989.005, L1016.020]

#### Further details

- Choose benefits based on what the target customer believes, such as concern for the environment, rather than on what the seller personally believes. A customer avatar built at the start of the process shows which beliefs and problems matter to the buyer. [L0396.024]

- A marketing message has five parts: a primary promise, a unique mechanism, dimensionalized benefits, a call to action and proof points. Each product's description should contain all five, because together they explain the promise, why it works, what the customer gains, what to do next and why to believe it. [L0399.009]

- Check whether existing product descriptions include all five marketing message parts. Missing parts are a gap to fix in the next step, not a reason to stop. [L0399.010]

If an external writer creates the product descriptions and marketing messages:
1. Share the unique mechanism with the writer.
2. Give the writer the marketing message builder information for each product they are writing about.
This helps them write accurate descriptions.
[L0399.012]

## Homepage, store look and conversion presentation

### Homepage headline and hero

- The first thing a visitor reads should say what the store does for them or what result they get, not what it sells. People buy a product to solve a problem or reach a goal, so the message should name that outcome. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.003]
- The homepage headline should state the business's primary promise. When the wording changes, for example for a new product launch, the core promise stays the same. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.004]
- Headline pattern: name the result the customer wants, then the specific worry the store removes, in one short line. The visitor sees their own goal first, so the page is about them rather than the store. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.043]
  - "Keep your gear dry on every trip, with a seal that never leaks." [L0409.043]
  - "Sleep through the night on a mattress that keeps your back supported." [L0409.043]
- Show the store's unique selling proposition in the top line or header so it is visible from the first moment. Keep it short and specific enough to set the store apart from competitors. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.005]
- Use sales language with some personality and explain how the business serves the customer. A page that only pushes sales and promotions makes the store look like a commodity, especially in a competitive market. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.039]
- Match the hero image with a statement of what you help the customer achieve, such as a travel experience. A sale banner placed over an aspirational image sends a conflicting message. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.040]
- In the first view, use a lifestyle image, a UGC image of a person holding the product, or a high-quality product photo. The visual style should match the brand shown in the ad, so visitors who land from TikTok or Facebook feel they are on a safe, recognisable store. [L0916.009]
- Organic short-video content is a main discovery channel, so many visitors arrive after watching raw, creator-made content. Real-customer photos match what they have just seen. [L0916.039]
- Store name and purpose:
  - A store name should signal what the store sells. If the name suggests a gift shop, the homepage, navigation and header must confirm gift buying, because visitors judge the purpose from the name and logo first. (Older source, Source A 2019-2022 range not stated; no year given.) [L0355.001]
  - Identify whether a visitor is buying for themselves or for someone else, because that changes the homepage structure and header messaging. For gift stores, the recipient (him, her, mother, father, children, grandparents) should be visible at the top of the page. [L0355.002]
- Display the best-selling category first, so visitors immediately see what the store is known for. Example: a jewelry store whose homepage is filled with bracelets because bracelets are its top seller. [L0838.014]
- Authority markers such as recognised media logos build trust and work best high on the homepage, where visitors look early rather than far down the page. [L0524.013]
- Visuals convey ideas and build a mental picture faster than descriptive text alone, because people process images as one of their primary senses. Use imagery alongside copy in ads, product pages and brand assets. [L0755.001]
- If you own a physical store, use a photo of the storefront with its signage as the homepage hero image, and show products with short demo videos. (Older source, 2019.) [L1002.016]
- Order of work: focus on the offer and the placement of the offer before the look of the site. Improve targeting and the offer first, then adjust the presentation on the website. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0408.012]

### USP block and trust claims

- USP block pattern: three to four short, bold lines, each stating one trust point (origin, safe ingredients, guarantee, reviews), with one line of supporting detail, placed in generous white space. It works because visitors scan for a reason to trust the store, and a quick scan should answer that without reading. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.041]
  - "Made in a small family workshop. Every batch is tested for purity before it ships." [L0409.041]
  - "Free shipping on every order. Not happy? We cover the return shipping." [L0409.041]
- Put the trust claims that your specific buyers care about most into the USP block, such as cruelty-free, vegan, aluminum-free, safe-ingredient or made-in-a-country statements. Each claim answers an objection the buyer would otherwise have to research on their own. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0409.053]

### Brand identity and consistent design

- Consistency before decoration:
  - Standardize all badges and logos to one color scheme, one font family and one visual style before launch. Even a new store should look like an established brand rather than a small improvised shop. [L0921.002]
  - Badges, icons and product images pulled from different sources, with mixed colors, fonts and styles, make a store look unprofessional and lower trust. Visitors judge this within seconds of arriving. A consistent visual system signals that real money went into the business. [L0921.001]
  - Large established retailers with one color scheme and one font system are a practical visual benchmark. Matching that level of consistency makes a small store look like a serious business from its first visit. [L0921.036]
  - Weak branding such as an amateur logo, clashing color combinations, mismatched fonts or an unchanged default theme makes a store look untrustworthy. Inconsistent design across pages signals that the store may not be legitimate. [L0390.024]
  - A consistent, distinctive design that competitors do not copy makes a brand recognisable at a glance, and that recognisability adds to perceived uniqueness. Choose one visual signature and apply it everywhere the buyer sees the brand. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L0411.035]
- Theme and sections:
  - Each homepage section with a Color scheme option (slideshow, collection list, featured collection, email signup, pre-footer) takes its colours from the palette set in Theme settings > Colors. Changing a section's scheme recolours only that section, which keeps the whole store consistent. [L1309.068]
  - Test the logo, fonts and colors on the live product page before finalizing. Brand fonts and colors usually need trial and error, so expect to revisit the branding once the product images and copy are in place. [L1331.008]
- Voice and copy:
  - The hero and feature copy should match the brand's own voice and niche, not the template's default category. When a template was written for another niche, rewrite headlines and body copy so each sentence fits the product's actual use. [L1188.039]
  - When mocking up a layout, use neutral placeholder text such as lorem ipsum. Real words on a theme can push the look of the design toward that topic and distort the choices. [L0659.047]
- **Conflict: badge colours.** L0921.002 says to standardize all badges to one colour scheme. L1188.040 says to use the brand colour and logo consistently across header, footer and rating badges, but where a third-party badge is shown, use that company's official colour so it looks authentic. Apply both: your own badges and logos follow the brand scheme; third-party badges keep their official colour. [L0921.002, L1188.040]

### Store look: branded versus generic dropshipping

| Signal | Generic store | Branded store |
|---|---|---|
| Overall impression | Empty, no brand personality, reads as a quick dropship store [L0657.038] | Branding, consistent colors and some character added [L0657.038] |
| Niche position | Copies a competitor's store; in niches with 20 to 100 or more sellers per product, it cannot stand out [L0403.002] | Branded landing page and store design beyond the product itself [L0403.002] |
| Packaging and assets | Unbranded | Branded boxes, a sample product photographed for the site, a custom-branded landing page [L1469.008] |
| Visuals | Generic product images | Professional branded images, lifestyle photos, custom packaging mockups [L1381.053] |

- A product page built with a branded template structure signals a professional store, even when using a template. Higher perceived trust raises conversion compared with minimal store designs. [L0321.040]
- A simple, low-excitement product can sell at a premium if the product page and visuals look clean and branded. Consistent photos, restrained text, benefit copy and a small discount or bundle make the store look trustworthy. [L0692.041]
- Match layout and tone to the price and nature of the product. A premium product needs a refined, confident look and tone; a bargain needs a busy, deal-focused look and tone. [L1366.112]
- For jewelry, keep the design light: few elements, elegant fonts, plenty of space, simple benefit text. Testimonials and trust signals stay. Features and comparison blocks can be removed if they add no real differentiator (e.g. anti-scratch or rust-proof material). [L1363.028]
- Invest little money, but make the look distinct. Custom branding raises conversion rates and separates the store from competitors. [L1469.008]

### Imagery and visuals

- Brand images should mix raw images, lifestyle images and review visuals showing people who are happy with the product. This gives the impression of a real, trusted company rather than a generic dropshipping store. [L0871.058]
- Sources for lifestyle images: Canva and Printful mockups, searches for lifestyle product images, or freelancers hired on Fiverr. Lifestyle images show people holding or using the product. [L1381.055]
- Influencer-style photography tends to devalue a premium brand image, while editorial-style photography supports higher pricing. The first impression a customer gets of the brand shapes how they see it afterwards. [L0561.025]
- Phone-shot product videos are good enough if the camera is held steady and the lighting is decent. Show how the product works, such as how a lock opens or how doors move. (Older source, 2019.) [L1002.017]
- Attaching artisan videos or interviews adds value but works best after the buyer finds the product. For buyers searching generic product names, optimize product pages and the Merchant Center feed for those terms first, then use videos for email or remarketing. [L0941.054]
- **Tension: polished versus raw imagery.** Use the source that fits the case:
  - Premium pricing: editorial style supports higher prices. [L0561.025]
  - Visitors arriving from short-video content: polished studio imagery can feel out of place; real-customer photos match what they have just seen. [L0916.039]
  - Default brand image set: a mix of raw, lifestyle and review visuals. [L0871.058]

### Product page trust elements

- Product pages need more than one kind of trust signal, because buyers scan images and GIFs before reading text. Combine visuals, reviews with photos, and direct answers to common concerns in the product description. [L0773.067]
- Product pages should answer the questions customers have before buying, so descriptions go beyond supplier stock text. Good product information gives customers the experience that makes them buy from you. [L0806.058]
- Show star ratings such as 4.8 out of 5 near the top of the product page. Visitors look at what other customers say first, so reviews should appear high on the page. [L1345.038]
- Reviews and photos of buyers who look similar to the shopper raise the perceived likelihood of achieving the dream outcome. A product page with only a product image and no reviews or wearers makes purchase decisions much harder. [L0502.005]
- Place a "guaranteed safe checkout" trust badge directly below the add-to-cart call-to-action button. Custom icons can also work if they are consistent with the brand. [L1345.043]
- Once reviews exist, enable a Shopify review app and configure Google Webmaster Tools so Google crawls regularly and detects new reviews. Star ratings then appear in Google search results next to your store name. (Older source, 2020.) [L0245.049]
- Advertise on the store which brands you sell for, the way large retailers state the brands they carry. Visitors arrive searching for those products, so the brand names help them decide. (Older source, 2021.) [L0834.114]
- Keep the product page estimate for lead time or shipping visible and clear. Customers usually accept longer delivery times when they are stated openly. (Older source, 2021.) [L0671.176]

### Footer

- Make the footer clean, professional and supplier-ready. Suppliers and wholesale partners evaluate store credibility partly through footer design and completeness. [L0102.011]
- The footer should contain:
  - the logo
  - About Us information
  - contact details
  - policy pages
  - an FAQ or how-to section
  - a short slogan or brand mission
  - a customer support email
  - links to social media profiles
  [L1326.011]
- Replace the Twitter bird icon with X: the Twitter icon in the Manhattan theme footer should be updated to X. (2024.) [L0836.049]
- Do not offer competitor referrals or recommendations on your store before the customer has purchased. Add referral recommendations only after purchase, to encourage repeat visits. (Older source, 2020.) [L0245.048]

### Cart and checkout

- Cart wording should match the local language of the market. Cart title text is free to edit, so merchants selling in the UK can use words like bag or basket instead of cart. [L1231.015]
- The cart icon in Shopify theme settings under Icons can be changed from Bag to Shopping cart. This is a matter of personal preference; the cart icon is often preferred because customers recognise it. (Older source, 2021-22.) [L1371.017]
- Progress bar message pattern: state the remaining amount to the next threshold, then name the reward. This tells shoppers exactly how close they are and what they gain. [L1231.059]
  - "Add $25.00 more to unlock a free tote bag." [L1231.059]
  - "You are $15.00 away from free shipping on your order." [L1231.059]

### Mobile

- Around 80% to 85% of visitors arrive on mobile, and they have very little time to decide. The hero image takes the most space above the fold, so it must convince quickly. [L1345.037]

## About Us page and brand story

### Structure and Content of the About Us Page

#### Mandatory Elements

An About Us page for an ecommerce store must include the following: store name, niche product category, city location, a statement of nationwide or multi-regional presence, global shipping carriers (FedEx, UPS and First Class Priority Mail are standard), years or duration in business, team existence or operational strength, and a clearly linked Contact Us page for customer questions [L0093.001].

Before customers place an order, they decide whether to trust the business. The About Us page is the primary tool for building confidence that you are a legitimate, established retailer [L0145.001].

The About Us page builds trust with visitors who do not know the store yet. It should be written carefully because new buyers often read it before deciding to purchase [L1130.003].

Shoppers look at About Us pages mainly when they do not know the store, such as smaller or niche brands. A bland statement about what you sell weakens trust, while a photo of the founder or team and a personal voice makes buying more likely [L1522.033].

#### Length, Tone and Wording Rules

- For a Shopify store selling high-ticket products, the About Us page should run 400 to 700 words, written as natural prose, with bullet points only where they make a point clearer [L0533.001].
- Never use the word "dropshipping" on an About Us page. Write as a serious, long-term retail brand with an established tone, even if the business is new. Avoid generic template phrasing and any startup or hobby-store feel [L0533.002].
- Older advice (Source A, 2020): the About Us page does not need an inspirational story or a made-up narrative. Focus on an educational approach instead: product benefits, the market gap you fill, and your commitment to service and quality [L0268.034, older].
- The About Us page should tell a story that ties the store to the products and the audience of the niche, without lying, and should speak to the niche's customers directly [L0359.063].
- High-ticket buyers need to feel safe before they pay. Build trust through clear supplier standards, service promises and delivery reliability. For this buyer group, confidence in these areas matters more than sales language [L0533.003].
- If the brand is new, the years-in-operation statement can say "many years of combined industry experience" instead of a specific number of years. Use only experience the owner actually has [L0533.004].
- A store that carries a person's name must make clear who that person is and what their role is. Visitors cannot tell what the brand stands for without this clarity [L0838.007].

#### Recommended Structure

1. Strong brand opening: who we are, who we serve, and what makes us different [L0533.005].
2. Mission and values: why the brand exists [L0533.005].
3. What we specialize in: products, quality, and supplier standards [L0533.005].
4. Customer experience and service promise [L0533.005].
5. Logistics and fulfillment confidence: shipping partners, reliability, and nationwide reach [L0533.005].
6. Closing paragraph with a soft call-to-action [L0533.005].

Source B's guidance (2021 to 2022, older) adds that the page should be personal and specific: who you are, why the company is worth buying from, how you serve your market, your USP and your primary promise [L1522.032, older].

#### Founder Story, Owner and Photo

- The About Us page must tell a story: who owns the business, why they are experts in the niche, and a photo of the owner or a person representing the company. Its purpose is to build trust before the customer buys [L1293.012].
- Create a brand story or about page that explains who runs the store and why it sells these products. It does not need to be long, but it helps visitors connect with the store [L1325.013].
- The About Us page should use a real photo of the founder, a staff member or someone using the product, plus a personal story about why the store exists. A generic stock template page does not build the same trust [L0910.019].
- Showing the owner's face and telling the origin story builds loyalty, because customers trust a visible founder more than an anonymous store selling an item they have not made or checked [L0871.057].
- Story formula: tell the brand's story in a few sentences covering where it started, what it is committed to, and the impact it wants to have. A clear purpose builds customer trust [L1071.018].
  - Example: a founder who wanted better tools for home bakers and committed to safer materials [L1071.018].
  - Example: a team that started a repair service and committed to reducing waste [L1071.018].

### Founder-Led and Curator-Style Stores

#### Choosing the Role

1. Decide whether the named person is the designer of the products or a curator who selects products from different brands [L0838.008].
2. Make the person the visible face of the business, with the chosen role stated clearly [L0838.008].
3. Build an About page with a photo of the person, why they are passionate about the product category, and why they design or select the products [L0838.008].
4. Tell the founder story on the About page so it explains why the store was built [L0838.008].
5. Use real photos of the person. If none exist, use a model or stock images, but turn the store into a real personal brand [L0838.008].

#### Personal Founder Brand Pattern

A personal founder brand follows this pattern: a named person appears in photos and videos, has a bio page telling their story, and the site content centres on that person. It works because visitors trust a face and a story more than an anonymous product catalogue [L0838.009].

- Example 1: a skincare store where the founder appears in her own videos and writes a blog about her routine [L0838.009].
- Example 2: a jewellery store where the owner, a self-taught designer, tells how she started making pieces for her friends [L0838.009].

#### Curator Product Pages

On a curator-style store with a named person, product pages should include that person's own short assessment of each item and why it is worth buying. This supports the expert role and gives visitors a reason to trust the selection [L0838.022].

### Honesty and Accuracy Rules

- Only list differentiators that are true today in the About Us prompt. Claims such as better warranties should be removed until the suppliers providing them are confirmed, because the AI will otherwise write them into the page as facts [L1048.009].
- Never invent a founder story to fill the optional field. If you have no real story, delete the entire section [L1048.011].
- A story must not be made up. It should tie the store to real products and the real niche audience [L0359.063].
- AI-generated About Us and policy text is a starting draft. The store owner must review it before publishing and check it against real business terms, because the text is written to sound professional rather than to match supplier terms [L1048.012].
- Production footage should match the brand's main selling point. Eco-focused brands can use factory footage and tech pack visuals as part of the story. Other brands should focus on the quality of the final product. Showing production works best when a factory offers video production or when you film the received product yourself in close-up detail [L1438.055].

### Shopify Setup

#### Creating the Page

1. Go to Shopify Admin > Online Store > Pages and click Add page [L0509.007].
2. Name the page About Us [L0509.007].
3. Keep the theme template on default page, because the About Us page does not need a contact form [L0509.007].
4. Paste your About Us text into the content area and edit it [L0509.007].
5. Click Save, then click View Page to check the result [L0509.007].

#### Adapting a Template Before Publishing

1. Replace the generic brand name with your store name [L0509.008].
2. Remove bold formatting from the store name if the rest of the text is not bold [L0509.008].
3. Replace the city with the city where the store is based, or delete the location sentence if you have no base yet [L0509.008].
4. Keep the worldwide shipping sentence only if you work with suppliers that ship internationally, and name the carriers you actually use (the template mentions FedEx, UPS and First Class Priority Mail). Otherwise change or delete it [L0509.008].
5. Replace the years-in-business line with your own experience, or delete it if you have none [L0509.008].
6. Read the whole text and remove any leftover template wording [L0509.008].

Leaving template text, the sample brand name or placeholder years on an About Us page makes the store look unfinished to a supplier reviewing it. Check every template sentence and either adapt or delete it [L0509.009].

### Template Texts

The following templates are from the course source. Replace each bracketed placeholder with real details, or delete the sentence if the detail does not apply, and check every sentence against the honesty rules above [L0093.002, L0093.003, L0093.004, L0093.005, L0093.006, L0145.002].

```
We operate an online retail shop offering competitive prices on [niche products]. Based in [City] with nationwide offices, we ship worldwide via FedEx, UPS, and First Class Priority Mail. Operating for [Time] years, we've built a solid reputation for delivering quality products and reliable customer service. Our team is focused on growing the business while maintaining the standards our customers expect. Use our "Contact Us" page for product questions or availability checks.
```
[L0093.002]

```
An e-commerce specialist serving the [specialty items] market from [Hometown] with nationwide and global reach. We rely on FedEx, UPS, and First Class Priority Mail for shipping. Our [Duration]-year track record in digital retail reflects our team's commitment to quality products and customer support. We're actively expanding our catalog and marketplace footprint to serve more customers. Questions about our products? Visit our "Get in Touch" page.
```
[L0093.003]

```
Your online destination for [specialized products], combining quality with customer focus. Located in [City], we serve customers nationwide and globally through FedEx, UPS, and First Class Priority Mail. We've spent [Time] years building an online reputation for excellence in our niche, driven by a dedicated team committed to expansion and continuous improvement. Explore our collection or contact us through the "Contact Us" page.
```
[L0093.004]

```
A niche retailer specializing in premium [niche] products that emphasizes both product curation and customer service. With headquarters in [City] and a national presence, we ship worldwide via FedEx, UPS, and First Class Priority Mail. Our [Time]-year presence in online retail has established us as an authority in the space. Our team is dedicated to offering the finest products to improve the shopping experience. For product availability or inquiries, use our "Contact Us" page.
```
[L0093.005]

```
A leader in the [niche products] market known for quality, fair pricing, and personalized service. We're based in [City] with nationwide operations and serve customers worldwide via FedEx, UPS, and First Class Priority Mail. Our [Time]-year history demonstrates our commitment to excellence, backed by a dedicated team constantly working to deliver the best products and experience. Contact our "Contact Us" page with questions or to check availability.
```
[L0093.006]

```
We sell [our niche products] online at competitive rates while prioritizing a pleasant shopping experience through quality items and responsive service. Our operation is headquartered in [Your City] with distribution points nationwide. We fulfill orders globally via FedEx, UPS, and First Class Priority Mail. We've been in business for over [Time] and have earned a solid reputation. Our team continues expanding inventory of the best [our niche products] available. Customers can reach us through the "Contact Us" page for product questions or availability checks.
```
[L0145.002]

### AI Prompt for Generating the Page

#### Steps

1. Copy the full About Us prompt from its copywriter role line to the end [L1048.007].
2. Paste it into ChatGPT or another AI tool [L1048.007].
3. Replace every example value in the student inputs section with your own details: brand name, website domain, primary niche or category, target customers (the ideal buyer), brand personality, city where the business is based, years in operation, what makes the store different, customer service philosophy, shipping and fulfillment style and founder story [L1048.007].
4. Delete the optional years-in-operation field if the business is new, and delete the founder story section if you do not want one [L1048.007].
5. Send the prompt and wait for the full output [L1048.007].
6. Copy the generated About Us text, read it through, and edit anything that does not match your store before pasting it into Shopify [L1048.007].

#### Inputs

- The brand personality input takes two to four traits, which can be picked from the suggested list or written in your own words. For example, a premium and fun positioning uses the two traits premium and fun [L1048.008].
- Example for a new single-niche store that works with domestic suppliers: niche specialization as a differentiator, fast response times, an educated and focused service approach, long-term customer relationships, domestic suppliers, insured delivery and tracked shipping. Freight, professional freight and warranties are removed when they do not apply yet [L1048.010].

#### Prompt Text

```
Write an About Us page for an online shop that sells high-ticket goods sourced from domestic suppliers. Act as a seasoned eCommerce brand copywriter. The page should feel established, reliable, customer-first and premium, even if the business is new. Never use the word dropshipping, and avoid any startup or hobby tone, so it reads like a long-term retail brand. Write in English with a professional, confident, warm, trust-building and premium voice. Cover these parts in order: an opening that explains who the brand is, who it serves and what sets it apart; a mission and values section that explains why the company exists; a section on specialties, covering the product range, quality and supplier standards; a section on customer experience and the service promise; a section on logistics and fulfillment, covering shipping partners, reliability and nationwide reach; and a closing paragraph with a soft call to action. The total length must be 400 to 700 words. Use flowing prose, and only use bullet points if they clearly improve readability. The text must sound human, not templated. If an optional input is empty, leave it out completely and do not leave any bracketed text. Mention the City or Region in the opening and the closing only, not repeatedly. Inputs to work with: the brand name; an optional website domain; the niche or product category (for example luxury outdoor furniture, massage chairs, surfboards, carports); the target customer (for example homeowners upgrading their outdoor space, wellness-focused professionals, surfers); 2 to 4 personality traits, chosen from options such as Premium, Friendly, Rugged, Minimalist, Family-Focused, Technical, Luxury, Relaxed or Professional; the City or Region (for example San Diego, Austin, Denver, Charleston); years in operation, which can be stated as over 5 years, nearly a decade, or many years of combined industry experience if the brand is new; differentiators (for example US-only suppliers, hand-selected products, expert support, faster shipping, higher quality, better warranties, niche specialization); the service philosophy (for example white-glove support, fast response times, education-focused, long-term relationships); the shipping and fulfillment style (for example domestic suppliers, professional freight, insured delivery, tracked shipping); and an optional founder story about why this niche and this business. Output only the finished About Us copy, ready to paste into Shopify, with no placeholders, no explanations, and wording that is different every time it is generated.
```
[L0533.006]

### Review Before Publishing

- AI-generated text is a starting draft. Check every claim against real supplier terms, shipping carriers, years in business and the founder story before publishing [L1048.012, L1048.009, L1048.011].
- Remove every template sentence, sample name and placeholder that does not apply [L0509.008, L0509.009].

## Trust and legitimacy signals

### Trust foundations

- Trust comes mostly from professionalism, which shows in bugs, speed, design and service. Quick tricks and low-effort shortcuts do not build lasting trust. [L0390.023]
- Trust grows when you remove the reasons customers would write or call before buying. Professional design, correct grammar, a visible phone number, a strong About page, a clear contact page, personal product notes and trust badges all serve this goal. [L0910.017]
- Visitors to a new store trust it more when it is visibly linked to brands, companies or associations they already recognize, because authority transfers by association. A brand with no recognition can borrow credibility this way. [L1050.001]
- Trust factors such as reviews, video reviews and user-generated content reduce perceived risk because buyers see satisfied customers. They work as both social proof and risk reversal, so they belong in the offer. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0411.029]
- With a cold audience, trust grows when you educate people on the problem and the solution before you sell. Deep product knowledge and expert-backed proof make customers feel loyal to the brand. [L1377.050]

### Business identity: email, phone and address

**Business email**

- Use professional business email addresses, not personal accounts, for supplier communication. This establishes legitimacy and credibility and supports long-term business partnerships. [L0119.001]
- Business buyers can be recognized by their email domain. Orders from company addresses rather than generic free providers such as gmail.com suggest a B2B customer. Track these companies so you can list them as customers once you have enough of them. [L1050.018]

**Virtual address**

- Register a virtual business address to satisfy supplier approval requirements and appear legitimate. Do not display it prominently everywhere on the website; use it for approvals while keeping your privacy. (older advice, 2020) [L0268.077, L0268.035]
- Do not post a physical location everywhere on the site. (older advice, 2020) [L0268.035]
- If a customer asks for a showroom, answer: "We do not have a physical location; we ship direct from the factory." If the product needs touch to decide, ask suppliers for swatches or samples (about 2 x 2 inch pieces) to send to customers. (older advice, 2022) [L0665.071]

### Phone numbers and voicemail

**Showing a phone number**

- Display a phone number on your website to increase trust, even if you cannot answer every call. A visible phone number signals credibility to customers. (older advice, 2022) [L0249.044]
- Put a dedicated business phone number on the website and give it to suppliers instead of a personal mobile. Buyers expect a business to have a separate contact line, and a personal number lowers perceived trust. You can still answer business calls on your own mobile, since the business number only needs to look professional. (older advice, 2021-22) [L1198.002]
- Show the phone number on the website so visitors can call before buying. (older advice, 2022) [L0429.005]
- Show the phone number prominently in the header for expensive products. (older advice, 2020) [L0268.035]
- For business-to-business sales, show your phone number prominently on the trade page and on the quote request form. (older advice, 2021) [L0853.026]
- Open Phone (open phone.com) provides US virtual phone numbers for non-US residents doing business in the United States. It is the alternative to Mighty Call if you cannot obtain a domestic number. [L0251.028]
- Mighty Call is a US virtual phone number service. Source A members receive a discount code in the bonus section of the members area. [L0251.029]

**Voicemail**

- Use this voicemail greeting template:

```
Welcome, this is [Your Name] at [store name]. We're currently unable to answer your call because we're helping other customers. Please leave a message with your name, email address, phone number, and the purpose of your call-we'll get back to you as quickly as possible.
```

[L0249.045] (older advice, 2022)

- Set up voicemail to be delivered as an email transcript or text message. You see the customer's request immediately and can prioritize urgent calls, such as large orders, during business hours. (older advice, 2022) [L0249.046]

**Outsourcing phone support**

- Outsource phone support through a virtual call center billed per call or per minute, rather than paying for fixed full-day staff. This gives 24/7 coverage while you work another job. (older advice, 2022) [L0249.047]
- Design your support around calls, texts and direct answers rather than purely automated channels if your core buyers are older and prefer personal contact. Know your buyer profile before you choose your service style. [L1472.045]

### Presence beyond the website

- To look legitimate to customers who doubt a new store, build a presence beyond the website. (older advice, 2022) [L0429.005]
  1. Create and keep active a Facebook page, an Instagram account and a Pinterest profile. [L0429.005]
  2. Post a couple of times per week on each, without needing daily posting in large communities. [L0429.005]
  3. Show a phone number on the website so visitors can call before buying. [L0429.005]
- A store with no social media links looks like a burner store to visitors who check whether a real business stands behind it. Missing social profiles weaken trust. [L1346.027]

### Support and service promises

- State the support you actually provide, such as phone availability, email response time and shipping time. Competitors selling at a loss tend to fall behind on service, so accurate service statements build credibility. (older advice, 2022) [L0665.068]
- Respond to positive reviews sent by email. Do not respond to public positive reviews on judge.me. (older advice, 2022) [L0665.070]
- Respond publicly to negative reviews only when the business genuinely failed. Ignore abusive reviewers. (older advice, 2022) [L0665.070]

### Supplier logos and authorized retailer status

**Authorized retailer statements**

- Display that you are an authorized retailer for the brands you sell. This builds your own authority by association and tells customers the products are genuine. (older advice, 2022) [L0272.027]
- Show only an authorized retailer badge for a brand you are actually approved to sell. A badge on a product page of a brand you do not represent is a false claim. [L1050.004]
- Place the authorized retailer statement on that brand's product pages in the product description, for example "We are an authorized retailer for [brand]". [L1050.004]
- Show the authorized retailer badge on each brand's collection page in Shopify, with a statement that you are an authorized retailer for that brand. [L1050.005]
- Use this statement near the product title or price, not only in the footer:

```
We are an authorized [official brand name] retailer.
```

[L1281.055] (older advice, 2022)

- When a brand approves you as a reseller, it may send an "authorized retailer" image. If it sends only its logo, create the badge yourself by adding the words "authorized retailer" under the logo. [L1050.017]
- When you request reseller approval, be ready to explain how you plan to position the supplier's products on your site. Brand owners want to see the intended positioning before they agree. (older advice, 2021-22) [L1060.012]

**Creating the badge**

Creating an authorized retailer badge (procedure merged from two units that describe the same process; the 2021 version gives the same four steps):

1. Get the brand logo. [L1050.002]
2. Open Canva (canva.com; the free version is enough) and load the logo. Add the supplier logo. [L1050.002, L0962.041]
3. Add the words "authorized retailer" under the logo. [L1050.002, L0962.041]
4. Save or export the result as your badge image and place it on the store. [L1050.002, L0962.041]

(older advice, 2021 for L0962.041)

- Keep logos simple. Overloaded logos with cartoon characters, large circles and taglines underneath look messy and reduce trust. (older advice, 2021) [L0962.042]

**Logo placement**

- Show supplier logos on product pages, not in the footer on every page. The logo is most useful where the buying decision happens. Footer logos add many images that slow the site without helping sales. (older advice, 2022) [L1281.054]
- Display the brand names and logos of your suppliers prominently and include a "Shop by Brand" navigation section. This captures customers searching for those brand names directly and builds your authority as an authorized retailer. (older advice, 2022) [L0272.063]
- Supplier trust badges can go on product pages, with a different badge for each brand. [L0667.029]

| Where the badge or logo goes | Rule | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Product page, product description | "We are an authorized retailer for [brand]" for brands you represent | [L1050.004] |
| Product page, near title or price | Official brand name statement | [L1281.055] (older, 2022) |
| Brand collection page | Authorized retailer badge with a statement | [L1050.005] |
| Product page | One supplier trust badge per brand | [L0667.029] |
| Footer | Not used for logos (slows the site) | [L1281.054] (older, 2022) |

### Trade associations, B2B and customer logos

**Trade associations**

- If you or your business belong to a trade association, show that membership on the About Us page. For example, show an NRA membership for a store selling gun safes, or membership in the International Interior Design Association for a store selling curtains. New stores usually have no such membership unless the owner has one personally. [L1050.012]

**B2B trade page**

- For business-to-business drop shipping, add a trade page that shows companies you have worked with and offers bulk discounts. (older advice, 2021) [L0853.026]
- Include a quote request form that asks for name, email, phone and products of interest. (older advice, 2021) [L0853.026]

**Customer logos**

- Once businesses start buying, add their logos to a "Past Clients" or "To the Trade" section to build social proof. Add a logo only after the client has ordered, and build the section over time. (older advice, 2020) [L0961.014]
- Create an "Our Customers" page on Shopify only once you have business customers, not from day one. List the companies that have bought from you. Visible business clients signal that your business sells to other businesses and raises authority. [L1050.015]

### Reviews, testimonials and supplier reviews

**Supplier reviews**

- Use supplier reviews on your store only with permission. (older advice, 2021) [L0853.050]
  1. Ask the supplier for permission to use their reviews. [L0853.050]
  2. Once they agree, copy the approved reviews into your review app. [L0853.050]
  3. Never import reviews from other websites without permission. [L0853.050]
- To get early reviews without waiting for sales, first ask the supplier to share its existing review database for its products. Then use the lesson's method to request reviews from customers who buy from you. [L0941.057]

**Testimonials**

- Quoted testimonials can serve as a starting point before real reviews exist. Replace them with real customer testimonials once sales come in. [L1345.039]

### Press mentions

- If a brand has been featured in the press, mention it in the brand's collection page content, not on product pages. Add it in the collection SEO section. [L0667.031]

### Charity partnerships

**Choosing a partner**

- Charity Navigator is a free website for checking charities. Use it to confirm that a partner is a registered 501(c)(3) and to review its finances before you donate. [L0790.031]
- When choosing a charity partner, pick three candidates and list the pros and cons of each. Make sure the partner has no downside that could harm your brand or the local economy. [L0790.032]
- Start with a domestic or local charity rather than an international one when you are new. Local partners are easier to verify and can strengthen trust with domestic customers. [L0790.033]
- A good benchmark is that about 5% of donations go to administration and executive salaries. Check this before donating and review the partner's past use of funds. [L0790.034]

| Check | Benchmark or rule | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Registration | Registered 501(c)(3) | [L0790.031] |
| Admin and executive salaries | About 5% of donations | [L0790.034] |
| Number of candidates | Three, with pros and cons of each | [L0790.032] |
| Starting point | Domestic or local partner | [L0790.033] |

**Permission and wording**

- Mention a charity you support, whether as a fixed percentage of sales, a fixed amount per month or ad hoc donations, on the store only with the charity's explicit permission. Smaller charities or ones you already have a relationship with are more likely to agree. [L1050.013]
- Naming a charity on your store without its explicit permission is a mistake. Charities are careful about who they are associated with because of charity scams. Ask for approval before you show the association. [L1050.014]
- A holiday charity pledge should state the percentage of sales, the exact holiday period it applies to, and the named beneficiary with its logo. Limiting the pledge to a defined period makes it verifiable and avoids an open-ended claim. [L0355.024]
- Humorous or off-brand copy next to a trust element, such as a charity donation message, undermines its seriousness. Keep copy near trust signals consistent with the store's tone. [L0355.004]

**Donation amounts**

- The source gives two values for the per-sale donation, and they apply to different situations:
  - The minimum donation per sale was set at 50 cents in the campaign described. [L0790.035]
  - A start at 25 cents is enough to make a charitable claim credible. [L0790.035]
- Use 25 cents as a credible starting point, and the 50-cent minimum as the value set in the campaign described. [L0790.035]
- A single sales day that donated 100% of revenue, split across three charities after a hurricane, totaled $75,000. [L0790.035]

**Transparency and campaigns**

- Be transparent about your donations and tell customers how much is given, because this drives conversions. The message must be genuine: it should accurately describe the product and the donation, and never misrepresent the brand. [L0790.036]
- A collaboration framed around social good, such as a promised donation match within a 24-hour window, earned coverage from major publishers. You do not need to announce final donation totals up front, but be ready to be transparent when asked. [L0790.037]
- Brands with a real cause tend to attract customers, especially when the cause is authentic. Profits can be donated, or customers can be offered an optional extra payment that goes to a specific charity or cause. (older advice, 2019) [L1068.076]
- Reading glasses donations were first routed through Vision Partners. A later distribution partner could handle about 200,000 pairs globally, shipped in batches of roughly 1,000 units. [L0790.102]

### Badges, claims and honesty

**Fake and purchased badges**

- Many dropshipping stores display fake or purchased trust badges, such as Google Trusted Store, Forbes, FDA approval or a TM trademark symbol. These badges can be bought cheaply, so they do not prove legitimacy, and you should not copy them. [L0372.042]
- Paid placements on sites such as Fox or Forbes can be bought through Brand Push Up. The lesson quoted about 195 to 200 USD for one featured placement. This is an optional, pricier way to gain authority. [L1363.012]
- Big-name brands can create brand association even when margins are low. Including them can strengthen brand recognition and a larger catalog. (older advice, 2019) [L1068.077]

**"Not sold on Amazon or eBay" badge**

- Some competitor product pages add a "not sold on Amazon or eBay" badge to signal that the brand sells only through its own store. Use this badge only if it is true for your supply, and check the marketplaces before publishing it. [L0600.037]

**Niche and generic trust badges**

- Trust badges that speak to the buyer's own world show that the store understands its customers and fits the niche theme. Generic badges, such as free shipping or a guarantee, fit any store, but a niche-specific badge builds credibility faster. Examples of the pattern: "Built by hikers for hikers" for a trail gear store, or "Tested by dog owners" for a pet bed store. [L1346.025]
- Show trust badges that cover shipping, product sourcing and a guarantee, such as free shipping, direct-from-manufacturer delivery and a 30-day guarantee. These help new visitors who do not know the brand yet. [L1346.026]

### Communicating about delays

- Send proactive customer communication. If delivery exceeds 2 weeks, email customers every 3 days with shipping status updates, explain the delay and confirm that a free gift is included. [L0324.021]
  1. Monitor shipment tracking daily. [L0324.022]
  2. If expected delivery exceeds 2 weeks, email the customer within the first week. [L0324.022]
  3. Explain the reason for the delay. [L0324.022]
  4. Confirm that a free gift is included in the parcel. [L0324.022]
  5. Send a follow-up email 3 days later with a tracking update. [L0324.022]

## Social media accounts and profile setup

### Strategy and platform priority

- Social accounts must be active before paid traffic is launched, so visitors who research credibility before buying find a complete brand presence. [L0040.011]
- Business social media accounts (Facebook and Instagram business pages) take about 30 minutes to set up and are required for most supplier approvals. [L0278.030]
- Social accounts work mainly through indirect sales. People who search for a store and find active social pages are more likely to trust it. [L0429.070] *(Older advice, 2022.)*
- Organic social with few followers is rarely worth the effort. Basic paid campaigns can build a few hundred likes for social proof. [L0429.070] *(Older advice, 2022.)*
- Expensive products are usually bought after research, so do not pay for social management to grow a large organic following. [L0470.087]
- Focus effort on Facebook and Instagram, the two primary accounts, instead of spreading effort across networks such as Twitter, Pinterest or YouTube (80-20 approach). [L0653.004]
- Facebook organic reach has been low for several years, so page posts are hard to show even to followers who like the page. Instagram offers more chance of virality and suits experimental, meme-style content. [L0653.015]
- Humorous meme-style content is an advanced step and is not needed at launch. Start with plain business updates such as products, promotions and restocks. [L0653.021]
- Instagram Reels help build a following for a new brand, since a few strong reels can gain a few hundred followers quickly. Do not rely on Reels alone for sales: in the owner's tests, Reels converted lower than TikTok, and the audience may be more skeptical. [L1077.037]
- Snapchat's audience spans a very wide age range, not only young users. It suits founder-led brands that want to connect personally with their audience. [L1504.018]
- Pinterest is covered in its own section below. [L1136.041]

### Profile photo, name, username and bio (all platforms)

**Profile photo**
- Use the same profile photo on every social platform so users recognise the account across apps. [L0915.003, L0915.009]
- The profile photo should clearly represent the brand or person, be eye-catching and colourful. [L0915.003]
- If the photo shows a face, zoom in so the face fills the circular avatar. On a phone the picture is tiny, so a far-away or off-centre shot and group photos are not recognisable. [L0915.004]
- A profile picture that cannot be identified at a glance while scrolling on a phone is a red flag. Use a clean, single-colour background with the subject clearly visible. [L0915.005]
- For a product or brand profile, use a high-quality product image, the product logo or the brand name, not a selfie. A profile video is optional. [L1380.010]
- Keep logos, colours and the profile photo consistent across all social pages, so users who find the brand on one platform recognise it on another. [L0915.009]

**Name and username**
- The account name (display name, not username) can be set to the store domain, for example brand.com, so people who tag the account see where to buy. [L1380.011]
- The Facebook page name should match the store name exactly, including the domain-style name the store uses, so customers can find the page when they search. [L0653.010]
- Use the store name as the Facebook page name, not the full website URL. [L0654.009]
- Instagram name should match the store name. [L0653.017]
- Usernames should match the store name, for example shakasurfboards. If the username is taken, try a close variation, for example shakasurfboard. [L0654.011, L0654.013]

**Bio: content**
- A bio should contain the main offer or where to shop, the location if the business has a physical location, the niche, a short catchy tagline and a contact email for brands or press. [L0915.006]
- Location in the bio strongly affects discoverability when people search for profiles, so include it when relevant. [L0915.007]
- A bio that only says "follow me", gives no information about the account and pushes people off the platform is weak. Give users a reason to follow instead of asking for it. [L0915.008]
- The platform reads the bio as a signal of what the page is about and which viewers to show it to. A bio that states the niche clearly helps the algorithm target the account, not only the human reader. [L0915.024]

**Bio: formula and examples**
- Bio formula: niche, then location (if there is a physical base), then a short catchy tagline that states the benefit, then a contact email for brand deals or press. A relevant emoji helps the bio stand out when scanned on a phone. [L0915.023]
- Example in the same pattern: `Vegan skincare for sensitive skin | Berlin 🇩🇪 | Routines that take 3 minutes ✨ | Brands: collab@yourbrand.com` [L0915.023]
- Second example in the same pattern: `Pet grooming tips for Labradors | Denver, CO 🐾 | New tip every Tuesday | Press: press@yourbrand.com` [L0915.023]

**Bio: length and structure (differing sources)**

| Source | Bio structure | When it applies |
|---|---|---|
| L0915.023 (Source E 2025-26) | Four parts: niche, location, tagline with benefit, contact email | Profiles where niche, location and contact all matter, for example brand deals or press |
| L1380.012 (Source C) | Two to three lines with a few emojis: line 1 slogan or key features; line 2 sale, discount or free worldwide shipping; line 3 pointer to the link (for example a downward-pointing emoji) | Store-focused profiles that push a sale or shipping offer |
| L0654.015 (Source A) | Instagram bio must be even shorter than the Facebook bio; trim until it fits | Instagram, compared with the Facebook page |

The template below uses the structure from L1367.006, with the bracketed parts replaced by your own details:

```
[Brand name]
[Shipping promise, e.g. Fast free tracked shipping worldwide]
[Social proof, e.g. +500 happy customers]
[Scarcity line, e.g. Limited stock available]
[Call to action, e.g. Buy using the link below]
```
[L1367.006]

### Facebook page setup

**Overview**
- Quick path to create a Facebook page: log in, click the plus (+) symbol at the top, select Page, complete the setup wizard, and the page is ready. [L0653.014]
- Overview of steps: create a Facebook business page; upload the business information (profile photo, cover image and about-us section); post two to three times a week; create an Instagram business account; upload its business information; post on Instagram two to three times a week. [L0653.005]

**Create the page (alternative path)**
- Create a new public Facebook Page by opening the Facebook profile switcher, choosing See all profiles, and then selecting Create Page. [L0651.009]
- On the Create a Page form, the Page name is required and should be the brand name. The Category field is also required and should be the category that best describes the business. The Bio field is optional and can be filled in later. [L0651.010]

**Create the page (full step list)**

1. Go to Facebook, click See more in the left navigation and click Pages. [L0654.010]
2. Click Create page. [L0654.010]
3. Choose Public page and click Next. [L0654.010]
4. Click Get started. [L0654.010]
5. Enter the store name as the page name, not the full URL. [L0654.010]
6. Choose a relevant category, for example Sporting goods store. [L0654.010]
7. Write a short bio based on the About Us page and paste it into the bio field, which appears under the profile picture and header. [L0654.010]
8. Click Next to the next page and enter the live store URL as the website. [L0654.010]
9. Enter the business phone number as the phone number. [L0654.010]
10. Enter the store email address, for example sales@ followed by the store domain. [L0654.010]
11. Leave the address empty; it is not necessary. [L0654.010]
12. Set the hours to Always open, because the store is e-commerce only. [L0654.010]
13. Upload the logo as the profile picture and the wide homepage header image as the cover photo. [L0654.010]
14. If the logo does not fit the profile picture, crop the symbol out of the logo so it fits in the middle. [L0654.010]
15. Click the action button, choose Learn more, enter the homepage URL as the link and click Save. [L0654.010]
16. Skip the WhatsApp number and skip inviting friends. [L0654.010]
17. Click Next, turn on emails about updates to the page and click Done. [L0654.010]

Steps 1 to 17 [L0654.010]

**Finish the page**
- After creating the page, upload the brand profile picture and the banner (cover photo), add a short store description and add the website link. [L0651.011]
- The Links section of a new Facebook Page accepts up to 10 links, such as websites, blogs or portfolios. [L0651.012]
- Edit page info via the sidebar option Edit Page Info. Use the store's logo as the profile photo and a cover image uploaded as the background. [L0653.012]
- Facebook image size requirements for the profile photo and cover image change over time. Check the current recommended sizes on a current source before uploading. [L0653.013]
- On the page, the account switcher at the top right toggles between your personal profile and any page you manage. To post as the business page, switch to the page and type in the post box while acting as that page. [L0654.024]

**Page fields**
- Fields: Name (must match store name), Username, Description (short synopsis of company and mission statement), Phone (optional, only if the business is live), Best contact email address, Best website, Address (only if a physical address exists). [L0653.011]
- Local-business fields such as service areas and opening hours can be skipped for an online store. Extras such as privacy policy and product information are not necessary at the beginning. [L0653.011]
- The bio should be a shorter version of the About Us page, and it must be short enough to show completely under the profile picture and header. [L0654.009]

**Page category (differing sources)**
- For an online store, select Product or service as the page category rather than a local business type. Local-business fields can then be left empty. [L0653.019]
- Example category used in the setup walkthrough: Sporting goods store. [L0654.010]
- The category is not the most important setting, but it is shown on the page, so pick one that matches the business. Facebook does not show every option in a complete list, so AI can help find a suitable category. [L0654.008]

**Prompts for the page bio and category**

Bio prompt:

```
I am creating the bio for a Facebook page for [your store name]. Here is the URL of the live store: [your store URL]. Please read the store and help me write a bio for the page. Please help me write this, and keep the bio short enough to fit in the bio section of the page without being cut off.
```
[L0654.006]

Category prompt:

```
What category should I use on a Facebook page for my store that sells [your product or niche]? Please suggest the most relevant categories for the page and briefly explain why each one fits.
```
[L0654.007]

**Facebook username**
- Username steps, while logged in as the page:

1. Click the three dots on the page. [L0654.011]
2. Click Settings and privacy. [L0654.011]
3. Scroll down the left navigation and click Page setup. [L0654.011]
4. Click the top link, Name. [L0654.011]
5. Click Edit next to the username. [L0654.011]
6. Enter the store name as the username, for example shakasurfboards. [L0654.011]
7. If the username is not available, try a close variation, for example shakasurfboard. [L0654.011]
8. Click Save and enter your password if asked. [L0654.011]
9. Check the page URL, which should now be facebook.com/ followed by the username. [L0654.011]

Path: Settings and privacy > Page setup > Name > Username > Edit > Save. The username appears in the page URL, so it should match the store name. A username can be unavailable if someone already used it, in which case use a close variation. [L0654.012]

### Instagram account setup

**Overview and fields**
- Quick path to create an Instagram business account: go to instagram.com while logged out, select Sign up, go through the signup process and choose the business account option, then upload business info and post two to three times a week. [L0653.016]
- Profile fields: Name (should match the store name), Username, Category, About us (short bio) and Website (link). These are edited via Edit profile. [L0653.017]
- Profile category, as listed in the fields: Brand for a store. [L0653.017]
- Category chosen when converting to a professional account: Shopping and Retail. Both are reported. The Brand value is the profile category, and Shopping and Retail is the category selected during conversion. [L0654.013, L0654.014]
- Instagram is mainly a mobile app, so posts are typically published from a phone. Posting from desktop is still possible using the create button, which accepts image uploads. [L0654.017]
- The website field can only be added in the mobile app, not on desktop. Log in to the account on a mobile device to enter the store website. [L0654.016]

**Create and convert the account**

1. Open instagram.com on desktop or use the mobile app. [L0654.013]
2. Click Create new account. Do not use your personal account, because this is a brand-new account for the store. [L0654.013]
3. Enter the store's official sales email address. If you cannot access the verification email, use another email address that you can access. [L0654.013]
4. Enter a password and a date of birth. [L0654.013]
5. Enter the business name as the full name. [L0654.013]
6. Enter the store name as the username. If it is taken, add a variation. [L0654.013]
7. Click Submit and enter the verification code sent to your email. [L0654.013]
8. Click More, then Settings. [L0654.013]
9. Scroll down the left navigation and click Account type and tools. [L0654.013]
